\end{overpic}

Figure 18
.
In the above figure, we have plotted
L_{1}\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket
(black curve) and
L_{2}\llbracket 2,2T\rrbracket
(blue curve). Endpoint tightness, Proposition
3.9
, ensures that
L_{1}(1),L_{2}(2)\in(-M_{0}\sqrt{T},M_{0}\sqrt{T})
. Assuming this, in order to seek an uniform upper bound for the blue curve, by stochastic monotonicity we may push the black curve all the way to
+\infty
. The resulting law for the blue curve is given by
Y(\cdot)
(upto a translation) introduced in Lemma
5.3
. A uniform upper bound for the resulting law for the blue curve law can then be estimated by Lemma
5.3
. The upper bound is shown in the dashed line above. Once we have a uniform upper bound for the blue curve, we may elevate the endpoints of black curve much higher (from black points to red points in the above right figure) so that the curve no longer feels the effect of the blue curve. The red curve above denotes a sample for
L_{1}
from this elevated end points. Without interaction with the blue curve, its law (upto a translation) equals to
X(\cdot)
in Lemma
5.3
. A uniform upper bound for the red curve can then be estimated by Lemma
5.3
.

Let us set
\mathcal{F}_{1}=\sigma(L_{2}(2),(L_{1}(i))_{i=1}^{2T-1})
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}=\sigma(L_{1}(1),(L_{2}(i))_{i=2}^{2T})
. In view of (
5.7
), by a union bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}(\mathsf{A}_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\mathbb{P}_{2}\bigg(\{L_{2}(2)\leq M_{0}\sqrt{T}\}\cap\mathsf{A}_{2}\bigg)\leq\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{L_{2}(2)\leq M_{0}\sqrt{T}}\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{2}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{1}\right]\right].

As
\mathsf{A}_{2}
is an increasing event with respect to the boundary data, due to stochastic monotonicity (Proposition
2.6
), increasing the boundaries will only increase the conditional probability of
\mathsf{A}_{2}
. Thus, to get an upper bound, we may assume
L_{2}(2T)=L_{2}(2)=M_{1}\sqrt{T}
, and
L_{1}(i)=+\infty
for all
i\in\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket
. Under this boundary condition we have
(L_{2}(i+1)-M_{1}\sqrt{T})_{i=1}^{2T-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}(Y(i))_{i=1}^{2T-1}
where
Y(\cdot)
is defined in Lemma
5.3
. Thus, almost surely we have (recall
\mathbb{P}
is the law of
Y
below)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{L_{2}(2)\leq M_{0}\sqrt{T}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{2}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{1}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{i\in\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket}|X(i)|\geq M_{0}\sqrt{T}\bigg)\leq\varepsilon.

Thus
\mathbb{P}_{2}(\mathsf{A}_{2})\leq 2\varepsilon
. In view of this bound, applying a union bound we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}(\mathsf{A}_{1})\leq 3\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{L_{1}(1)\leq M_{1}\sqrt{T}\}\cap\neg\mathsf{A}_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{2}\right]\right].

As
\mathsf{A}_{1}
is an increasing event with respect to the boundary data, due to stochastic monotonicity (Proposition
2.6
), increasing the boundaries will only increase the conditional probability. Thus, to get an upper bound we may assume
L_{1}(1)=L_{1}(2T-1)=M_{3}\sqrt{T}
and
L_{2}(i)=(M_{0}+M_{1})\sqrt{T}
for all
i\in\llbracket 2,2T\rrbracket
. From the definition of the Gibbs measure, almost surely we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{L_{1}(1)\leq M_{1}\sqrt{T}\}\cap\neg\mathsf{A}_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\Delta]}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}}\right],

where on the right-hand side,
\mathsf{A}_{1}
is defined as the event
\{\sup_{i\in\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket}X(i)\geq M_{0}\sqrt{T}\}
and

\Delta=\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{T-1}\left(e^{-(M_{0}+1)\sqrt{T}-X_{1}(2i-1)}+e^{-(M_{0}+1)\sqrt{T}-X_{1}(2i+1)}\right)\right).

As
\Delta\leq 1
, by (
5.3
),
\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\!\cdot\!\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}}]\leq\varepsilon
. By (
5.3
) we have
\mathbb{E}[\Delta]\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot e^{-2(T-1)e^{-\sqrt{T}}}\geq\beta
for some absolute constant
\beta>0
. Thus,
\mathbb{P}_{2}(\mathsf{A}_{1})\leq(3+\beta^{-1})\varepsilon
. Similarly one can show

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}\left(\inf_{i\in\llbracket 2,2T\rrbracket}L_{2}(i)\leq-(M_{3}+M_{0})\sqrt{T}\right)\leq(3+\beta^{-1})\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}\left(\inf_{i\in\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket}L_{1}(i)\leq-(M_{0}+M_{1})\sqrt{T}\right)\leq 2\varepsilon.

Thus adjusting the constants we can find
\widetilde{M}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}\bigg(\sup_{i\in\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket}|L_{1}(i)|+\sup_{j\in\llbracket 2,2T\rrbracket}|L_{2}(j)|\geq(M-1)\sqrt{T}\bigg)\leq\varepsilon/3.

Finally via Lemma
4.4
we know
L_{2}(1)-L_{2}(2)\sim G_{\alpha_{2}+\theta,1}
. Thus, by a union bound, for all
T
large enough we have

\mathbb{P}_{2}(|L_{2}(1)|\geq M\sqrt{T})\leq\varepsilon/3+\mathbb{P}_{2}(|L_{2}(1)-L_{2}(2)|\geq\sqrt{T})\leq 2\varepsilon/3
. By another union bound, we arrive at (
5.6
).
∎

Recall the normalizing constant
V_{p}^{T}(\vec{y},\vec{z})
from (
2.8
). One can easily obtain a lower bound for this normalizing constant as a consequence of the Lemma
5.4
.

Corollary 5.5
.

Fix any
r>0
and for each
N>0
set
T=\lfloor rN^{2/3}\rfloor
. Fix any
p\in\{1,2\}
and set
\alpha=\alpha_{p}
according to (
3.11
). Recall
V_{p}^{T}(\vec{y},\vec{z})
from (
2.8
). There exists
Q_{0}=Q_{0}(r)>0,N_{0}=N_{0}(r)>0
such that for all
Q\geq Q_{0}
and
N\geq N_{0}
,
V_{p}^{T}(\vec{y},\vec{z})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}
for all
\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{T}
with
z_{i}\leq QN^{1/3}
for
i\in\llbracket 1,T\rrbracket
and
\vec{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
y_{i}\geq(2Q-1)N^{1/3}
for
i\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket
. Here we assume
L_{p}(2T+1):=\infty
.

Proof.

Consider the event

\mathsf{A}:=\left\{\inf_{j\in\llbracket 1,T\rrbracket}L_{p}(2j-1)\geq(Q+1)N^{1/3}\right\}.

Observe that

\displaystyle V_{p}^{T}(\vec{y},\vec{z})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{T};p,T}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}}\prod_{j=1}^{T}W(z_{j};L_{p}(2j+1),L_{p}(2j-1))\right]

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-2Te^{-N^{1/3}})\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{T};p,T}\left(\mathsf{A}\right).

Taking
N
large enough ensures
\exp(-2Te^{-N^{1/3}})\geq 1/\sqrt{2}
. Since
\mathsf{A}
is an increasing event with respect to the boundary data, applying stochastic monotonicity (Proposition
2.6
) and translation invariance (Lemma
2.1

(a)
) of
\mathpzc{HSLG}
Gibbs measures we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{T};p,T}\left(\mathsf{A}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{T};p,T}\left(\inf_{j\in\llbracket 1,T\rrbracket}L_{p}(2j-1)\geq-(Q-2)N^{1/3}\right)

where
\vec{x}=0
if
p=1
and
\vec{x}=(0,-\sqrt{T})
if
p=2
. Appealing to Lemma
5.4
we may choose
Q
large enough so that the above probability is at least
1/\sqrt{2}
.
∎

5.2.
Proof of Proposition
5.1

For clarity we divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1.
In this step, we give a roadmap of the proof of (
5.1
) leaving the technical details to later steps. Fix
r,\varepsilon,\delta>0
and
p\in\{1,2\}
. Fix
N\geq 3
large enough so that
T=8\lfloor rN^{2/3}\rfloor\geq 24
. Set
\alpha=\alpha_{p}
according to (
3.11
) and consider the
\mathpzc{HSLG}
line ensemble
\mathcal{L}^{N}
from Definition
2.7
with parameters
(\alpha,\theta)
. Consider the modulus of continuity event

\displaystyle\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}:=\big\{\omega_{\delta}^{N}(\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N},\llbracket 1,T/4-1\rrbracket)\geq\gamma N^{1/3}\big\}.

By Theorem
3.10
, there exists
V=V(\varepsilon)>0
such that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{A}_{1})\geq 1-\varepsilon,\,\,\mbox{ where }\,\,\mathsf{A}_{1}:=\left\{N^{-1/3}|\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(1)|+N^{-1/3}|\mathcal{L}_{2}^{N}(2)|\leq V\right\}.

By Proposition
3.4
, there exists
M_{1}(\varepsilon)>0
such that for all large enough
N

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T-1)\geq M_{1}N^{1/3}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

We claim that there exists
M_{2}(r,\varepsilon)>0
such that for all large enough
N

(5.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}_{p}^{N}(2T+p-2)\leq-M_{2}N^{1/3}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

We shall prove (
5.10
) in
Step 2
. Let us assume it for now. Set
M=\max\{M_{1},M_{2},4\}
and consider the events

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T-1)|\leq 2MN^{1/3}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\mathcal{L}_{2}^{N}(2T)\geq-MN^{\frac{1}{3}},\,\,\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T-1)\leq MN^{\frac{1}{3}},\,\,\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T-1)\geq\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T)-(\log N)^{7/6}\Big\},

For each
\beta>0
we define

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)

\displaystyle:=\Big\{V_{p}^{T}\left((\mathcal{L}_{j}^{N}(2T+j-2))_{j\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket};(\mathcal{L}_{p+1}^{N}(2k))_{k=1}^{T}\right)\geq\beta\Big\},

where
V_{p}^{T}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
2.8
). We now claim that there exists
\beta(r,\varepsilon)>0
such that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)\right)\leq\varepsilon.

We work with this choice of
\beta
for the rest of this step. We postpone the proof of (
5.12
) to
Step 3
. Let us now complete the proof of Proposition
5.1
assuming it. Consider the following
\sigma
-algebra:

(5.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{p,k}:=\sigma\left((\mathcal{L}_{i}^{N}\llbracket 1,2N-2i+2\rrbracket)_{i\geq p+1},(\mathcal{L}_{i}^{N}(j))_{j\geq 2k+i-2,i\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket}\right).

Clearly
\mathsf{B}_{p}\cap\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)
is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{p,T}
. By union bound and tower property of conditional expectation we have

(5.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{A}_{1})+\mathbb{P}\left(\neg\mathsf{B}_{p}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{p}\cap\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\cap\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{p,T}\right]\right].

We bound the four terms on the right-hand side of the above equation separately.

\mathsf{A}_{1}
event:
We have
\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{A}_{1})\leq\varepsilon
due to (
5.8
).

\mathsf{B}_{p}
event:
Note that for large enough
N
,
\mathsf{B}_{2}\subset\mathsf{B}_{1}
. Combining (
5.9
), (
5.10
), and Theorem
3.1
(with
\rho\mapsto\frac{1}{2},M\mapsto M
), by a union bound we see that for all large enough
N
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{B}_{p})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{B}_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{L}_{2}^{N}(2T)\leq-MN^{1/3})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T-1)\geq MN^{1/3})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T-1)\leq\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2T)-(\log N)^{7/6}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+2^{-N}\leq 3\varepsilon.

\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)
event:
We have
\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta))\leq\varepsilon
due to (
5.12
).

Conditional probability:
By Theorem
1.3
and (
2.7
) we have

(5.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\cap\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{p,T}\right]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T};p,T}\left[V_{p}^{T}\left(\vec{y};(\mathcal{L}_{p+1}^{N}(2i))_{i=1}^{T}\right)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\cap\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}}\right]}{V_{p}^{T}\left(\vec{y};(\mathcal{L}_{p+1}^{N}(2i))_{i=1}^{T}\right)}

where
\vec{y}:=(\mathcal{L}_{j}^{N}(2T+j-2))_{j\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket}
and
V_{p}^{T}(\cdot;\cdot)
is defined in (
2.8
). From definition we have
V_{p}^{T}\left(\vec{y};(\mathcal{L}_{p+1}^{N}(2i))_{i=1}^{T}\right)\in[0,1]
. On
\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)}\cdot\mbox{r.h.s.~\eqref{e45}}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)}\cdot\beta^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T};p,T}\left(\mathsf{A}_{1}\cap\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\right).

Observe that the event
\mathsf{B}_{p}
ensures
\vec{y}\in I_{p,M}
where the set
I_{p,M}
is defined in the statement of Proposition
5.2
. We can thus apply Proposition
5.2
with
\varepsilon\mapsto\beta\cdot\varepsilon
, to get a
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{p}}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T};p,T}\left(\mathsf{A}_{1}\cap\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\right)\leq\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{p}}\cdot\varepsilon,

for all large enough
N
. Thus overall we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{p}\cap\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{A}_{1}\cap\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{p,T}\right]\big]\leq\varepsilon.

Plugging the above four estimates into r.h.s. (
5.14
) and taking limsup
N\to\infty
, then
\delta\downarrow 0
, yields

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta})\leq 6\varepsilon.

As
\varepsilon
is arbitrary, we thus have (
5.2
), completing the proof.

Step 2.
In this step we prove (
5.10
). We write
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}
instead of
\mathbb{P}
to stress the fact that the
\mathpzc{HSLG}
line ensemble has boundary parameter
\alpha_{p}
, defined in (
3.11
). We claim that there exists
M_{2}(r,\varepsilon)
such that for all large enough
N

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{p}^{(M_{2})}\right)\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{4},\quad\mathsf{Fall}_{p}^{(M_{2})}:=\left\{\inf_{j\in\llbracket 1,4T+4\rrbracket,i\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{i}^{N}(j)\leq-M_{2}N^{1/3}\right\}.

Note that as
\{\mathcal{L}_{p}^{N}(2T+p-2)\leq-M_{2}N^{1/3}\}\subset\mathsf{Fall}_{p}^{(M_{2})}
, (
5.16
) implies (
5.10
). To show (
5.16
), we first define a few more events. For each
R\geq 32r+1
we define

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{i}^{(R,j)}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathcal{L}_{i}^{N}(2j+i-2)\geq-R^{2}N^{1/3}\right\},\quad\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{i}^{(R,j)}:=\mathsf{B}_{i}^{(R,j)}\cap\bigcup_{k\in\llbracket j+1,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\neg\mathsf{B}_{i}^{(R,k)},

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{i}^{(R)}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\mathsf{B}_{i}^{(R,j)}=\bigsqcup_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{i}^{(R,j)}=\bigg\{\sup_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{i}^{N}(2j+i-2)\geq-R^{2}N^{1/3}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{Dif}^{(R)}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2j-1)\geq\mathcal{L}_{2}^{N}(2j)+(\log N)^{2}\mbox{ for all }j\in\llbracket 1,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket\right\}.

By Theorem
3.1
, Theorem
3.3
, and Proposition
3.4
, we can find a
R=R(r,\varepsilon)\geq 1
such that for all large enough
N
, and for
v\in\{1,2\}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{v}}\left(\neg\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(R)}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{v}}\left(\neg\mathsf{B}_{2}^{(R)}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{v}}\left(\neg\mathsf{Dif}^{(R)}\right)\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{8}.

We fix this choice of
R
. Observe that for large enough
N
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,i)}\cap\mathsf{Dif}^{(R)}\subset\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,i)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,i)},

uniformly for all
i\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket
. For
p=2
, by the union bound and the tower property of conditional expectation, in view of (
5.17
), we have

(5.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}\left(\neg\mathsf{B}_{2}^{(R)}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\neg\mathsf{Dif}^{(R)}\right)+\sum_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,j)}\cap\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{8}+\sum_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,j)}}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2})}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{2,j}\right]\right],

where
\mathcal{F}_{p,k}
is defined in (
5.13
). For
p=1
, applying union bound and using (
5.17
) we have

(5.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{1}^{(M_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}\left(\neg\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(R)}\right)+\sum_{j\in\llbracket 4T,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{1}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{Fall}_{1}^{(M_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{8}+\sum_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{1}^{(R,j)}}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{1}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Fall}_{1}^{(M_{2})}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{1,j}\right]\right].

We now proceed to control the conditional expectation
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{p}^{(M_{2})}\mid\mathcal{F}_{p,j}\right)
separately for
p=1
and
p=2
. Applying the Gibbs property (Theorem
1.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,j)}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}\left[\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2})}\mid\mathcal{F}_{2,j}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,j)}}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},\vec{z};2,j}\bigg(\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,j)}}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{(0,-\sqrt{j}),(-\infty)^{j};2,j}\bigg(\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2}-4R^{2})}\bigg).

Here
\vec{y}=(\mathcal{L}_{1}^{N}(2j-1),\mathcal{L}_{2}^{N}(2j))
and
\vec{z}=(\mathcal{L}_{3}^{N}(2m))_{m=1}^{j}
. Let us briefly explain the above inequality. Note that on
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{2}^{(R,j)}\cap\mathsf{B}_{1}^{(2R,j)}
we have
y_{i}\geq(-4R^{2}N^{1/3}-(i-1)\sqrt{j})
for
i=1,2
. Furthermore
\mathsf{Fall}_{2}^{(M_{2})}
is an event which decreases with respect to boundary data. Thus to obtain an upper bound, by stochastic monotonicity (Proposition
2.6
), we may take the boundary data from
(y_{1},y_{2})
to
(-4R^{2}N^{1/3},-4R^{2}N^{1/3}-\sqrt{j})
and
\vec{z}
to
(-\infty)^{j}
. The above inequality then follows by translation invariance (see Lemma
2.1

(a)
). Similar applications of the Gibbs property and stochastic monotonicity yield that on
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{1}^{(R,j)}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{1}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Fall}_{1}^{(M_{2})}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{2,j}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}^{0,(-\infty)^{j};1,j}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{1}^{(M_{2}-4R^{2})}\right).

We now claim that one can choose
M_{2}(r,\varepsilon)>0
large enough such that for all
j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket
,

(5.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{j};p,j}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{p}^{(M_{2}-4R^{2})}\right)\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{8},

where
\vec{x}:=0
(if
p=1
) or
\vec{x}:=(0,-\sqrt{j})
(if
p=2
). Plugging the above bound back in (
5.19
) and (
5.18
) and using the fact that
\{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}_{p}^{(R,j)}\}_{j\in\llbracket 4T+4,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket}
is a disjoint collection of events we arrive at the bound in (
5.16
). Thus we are left to verify (
5.20
) in this step. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{j};p,j}\left(\mathsf{Fall}_{p}^{(M_{2}-4R^{2})}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{j};p,j}\left(\inf_{k\in\llbracket 1,2j+i-2\rrbracket,i\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket}L_{i}(k)\leq-(M_{2}-4R^{2})N^{1/3}\right)

By Lemma
5.4
, one can choose
M_{2}
large enough such that the above expression is bounded above by
\varepsilon/8
for all
j\in\llbracket 4T,RN^{2/3}\rrbracket
. This proves (
5.20
) completing our work for this step.

Step 3.
In this step we prove (
5.12
).
For each
Q>0
consider the event

(5.21)

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{Q}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\sup_{i\in\llbracket 1,4T+4\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{p+1}^{N}(i)\leq QN^{1/3},\,\,\inf_{j\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{j}^{N}(4T+j+2)\geq-QN^{1/3}+\sqrt{2T+1}\right\}.

By Theorem
3.1
, Proposition
3.8
, and (
5.16
) there exists
Q(r,\varepsilon)>0
large enough such that
\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{D}_{p,Q})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
. Consider
\mathcal{F}_{p,2T+2}
from (
5.13
). Recall the event
{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta)
from (
5.11
). By union bound and the tower-property of the expectation, we have

(5.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)\cap\mathsf{D}_{Q})+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{D}_{Q}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{p,2T+2}\big]\Big]+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}.

Applying the Gibbs property and (
2.7
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}_{\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{p,2T+2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y};\vec{z};p,2T+2}\big(\neg\mathsf{C}(p,\beta)\big)

with
\vec{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{p})
and
y_{j}=\mathcal{L}_{j}^{N}(4T+j+2)
for
j\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket
, and
\vec{z}=(\mathcal{L}_{p+1}^{N}(2k))_{k=1}^{2T+2}
. Set
\vec{x}=(-QN^{1/3}+\sqrt{2T+1})^{p}
. We claim that there exists
Q_{0}(r,\varepsilon)>0
,
N_{0}(r,\varepsilon)>0
and
\beta(r,\varepsilon)>0
, such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
,
Q\geq Q_{0}
,
y_{i}\geq x_{i}
and
\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2T+2}
with
\sup_{i\in\llbracket 1,2T+2\rrbracket}z_{i}\leq QN^{1/3}
we have

(5.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y};\vec{z};p,2T+2}\left(\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta)\right)\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2},\mbox{ where }\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta):=\left\{\mathcal{V}_{p}\geq\beta\right\},

where we set (see (
2.8
))

(5.24)

\mathcal{V}_{p}:=V_{p}^{T}\big((L_{i}(2T+i-2))_{i\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket},(z_{1},\ldots,z_{T})\big).

Clearly in view of the definition of
\mathsf{D}_{Q}
from (
5.21
), the above claim shows that r.h.s. (
5.22
) is at most
\varepsilon
(
5.12
). Thus, to complete our proof it suffices to check (
5.23
). Towards this end, we first claim that for all
\vec{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2T+2}

(5.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y};\vec{z};p,2T+2}\left(\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta)\right)=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y};\vec{w};p,2T+2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta)}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{p}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{p}\right]}{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y};\vec{w};p,2T+2}\left[\mathcal{R}_{p}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{p}\right]},\quad\mbox{ where }\,\,\mathcal{R}_{p}:=e^{-e^{z_{T}-L_{2}(2T+1)}\mathbf{1}_{p=2}},

and where
\vec{w}\in[-\infty,\infty)^{2T+2}
is defined by setting
w_{i}=-\infty
for
i\leq T
and
w_{i}=z_{i}
for
i>T
. We postpone the proof of (
5.25
) to the next step.

Assuming (
5.25
), to prove (
5.23
), we provide upper and lower bounds for the numerator and denominator of r.h.s. (
5.25
) respectively. Consider the events

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{L_{1}(2T-1)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{L_{2}(2T)\geq 2QN^{1/3},L_{2}(2T+1)\geq 2QN^{1/3},L_{1}(2T-1)\geq(2Q-1)N^{1/3}\right\}

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},\vec{w};p,2T+2}\left[\mathcal{R}_{p}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{p}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},\vec{w};p,2T+2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{R}_{p}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{p}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{p}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\exp(-e^{-QN^{1/3}})\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{y},\vec{w};p,2T+2}\big(\mathsf{R}_{p}\big)

(5.26)

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\exp(-e^{-QN^{1/3}})\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{p}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{2T+2};p,2T+2}\big(\mathsf{R}_{p}\big).

where the penultimate inequality follows from the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{p}
and Corollary
5.5
and the final inequality follows via stochastic monotonicity (Proposition
2.6
) as
\mathsf{R}_{p}
is an increasing event with respect to the boundary data (recall
y_{i}\geq x_{i}
). To lower bound the above expression, we proceed into two cases depending on the value of
p
.

Case 1.
p=1
.
Note that
\mathsf{R}_{1}\supset\mathsf{RP}_{1,Q}
event defined in (
4.2
). By Proposition
4.1
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{2T+2};1,2T+2}(\mathsf{R}_{1})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}^{-QN^{1/3},(-\infty)^{2T+2};1,2T+2}(\mathsf{RP}_{1,Q})\geq\phi_{1}>0
for some
\phi_{1}
free of
N
.

Case 2.
p=2
.
Let
\vec{u}:=(-QN^{1/3}+\sqrt{2T+2},-QN^{1/3})
. Let us use the shorthand notation
\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}
for
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}),(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}
. Note that by stochastic monotonicity and union bound we have

(5.27)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{x}}(\mathsf{R}_{2})\geq\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}(\mathsf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}\Big(\{L_{2}(2T)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\}\cap\{L_{2}(2T+1)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\}\Big)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}\big(L_{1}(2T-1)\leq L_{2}(2T)-N^{1/3}\big).

Note that
\mathsf{RP}_{2,Q}\subset\{L_{2}(2T)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\}\cap\{L_{2}(2T+1)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\}
(with
T
replaced by
T+1
in (
4.2
)). Applying stochastic monotonicity (Proposition
2.6
) and Proposition
4.1
with
p\mapsto 2
and
T\mapsto T+1
, we see that the first term in the above equation can be bounded as

(5.28)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}\Big(\{L_{2}(2T)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\}\cap\{L_{2}(2T+1)\geq 2QN^{1/3}\}\Big)\geq\mathbb{P}_{2}^{(-QN^{1/3},-(Q+1)N^{1/3})}\big(\mathsf{RP}_{2,Q}\big)\geq\phi_{2},

for some
\phi_{2}>0
free of
N
. As for the second term in r.h.s. (
5.27
), by translation invariance (Lemma
2.1

(a)
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}\big(L_{1}(2T-1)\leq L_{2}(2T)-N^{1/3}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{2}^{(0,-\sqrt{2T+1})}\big(L_{1}(2T-1)\leq L_{2}(2T)-N^{1/3}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{2T+2;(0,\sqrt{2T+1})}\left[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}(T-1)\leq S_{2}(T-1)-N^{1/3}}\right]}{{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{2T+2;(0,\sqrt{2T+1})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]}},

where the last equality follows from Lemma
4.4
(recall the PRW law from Definition
1.7
and
W_{\operatorname{sc}}
from (
1.15
)). Now by Corollary (
4.12
),
{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{2T+2;(0,\sqrt{2T+1})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]\geq\mathrm{C}/\sqrt{2T+2}
for some absolute constant
\mathrm{C}>0
. However on the event
\{S_{1}(T-1)\leq S_{2}(T-1)-N^{1/3}\}
,
W_{\operatorname{sc}}\leq\exp(-e^{N^{1/3}})
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}\big(L_{1}(2T-1)\leq L_{2}(2T)-N^{1/3}\big)\to 0

as
N\to\infty
. Hence, inserting (
5.28
) back in r.h.s. (
5.27
), we see that for all large enough
N
,

\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{x}}(\mathsf{R}_{2})\geq\phi_{2}-\mathbb{P}_{2}^{\vec{u}}\big(L_{1}(2T-1)\leq L_{2}(2T)-N^{1/3}\big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\phi_{2}.

Summarizing the above two cases, for all large enough
N
, (
5.26
) is lower bounded by some
\phi>0
free of
N
. For the numerator in r.h.s. (
5.25
) observe that as
\mathcal{R}_{p}\leq 1
, by definition of the event
\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta)
, we have
\mathbf{1}_{\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(p,\beta)}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{p}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{p}\leq\beta
. Let us now choose
\beta=\phi\varepsilon
. Plugging these bounds back in r.h.s. (
5.25
) yields (
5.23
).

Step 5.
All that remains is to prove (
5.25
). We will do this for the
p=2
case. The
p=1
case is done analogously. Fix any
\vec{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2T+2}
and define
\vec{w}\in[-\infty,\infty)^{2T+2}
such that
w_{i}=-\infty
for
i\leq T
and
w_{i}=z_{i}
for
i>T
. Assume
(L_{1}\llbracket 1,4T+3\rrbracket,L_{2}\llbracket 1,4T+4\rrbracket)\sim\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},\vec{z};2,2T+2}
. Let
\mathcal{G}:=\sigma(L_{i}\llbracket 2T+i-2,4T+i+2\rrbracket)_{i\in\llbracket 1,2\rrbracket}
. Fix any event
\mathsf{F}
measurable with respect to
\mathcal{G}
. Set
L_{2}(4T+1)=\infty
. We claim that (recall
W(a;b,c)
from (
1.5
),
\mathcal{V}_{p}
from (
5.24
), and
\mathcal{R}_{2}
from (
5.25
))

(5.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{2T+2}{W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}\prod_{j=1}^{2T+2}{W(w_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\right].

Assuming (
5.29
) we can finish the proof of (
5.25
) (for
p=2
) via the following string of equalities:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};\vec{z};2,2T+2}\bigg(\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(2,\beta)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(2,\beta)}\cdot\prod\limits_{j=1}^{2T+2}W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))\bigg]}{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\bigg[\prod\limits_{j=1}^{2T+2}{W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\bigg]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(2,\beta)}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{2T+2}W(w_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))\bigg]}{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\bigg[\mathcal{R}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{2T+2}{W(w_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\bigg]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};\vec{w};2,2T+2}\bigg[\mathbf{1}_{\neg\widetilde{\mathsf{C}}(2,\beta)}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}\bigg]}{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y};\vec{w};2,2T+2}\bigg[\mathcal{R}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{2}\bigg]}.

Let us briefly explain the above equalities. The first equality is due to (
2.7
) and (
2.8
). In the second equality we have applied (
5.29
) to the numerator and denominator by taking
\mathsf{F}=\neg\mathsf{C}(2,\beta)
and
\mathsf{F}=\Omega
(the full set, i.e.,
\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{F}}=1
) respectively. The last equality follows by applying (
2.7
) and (
2.8
) again. This proves (
5.25
) modulo (
5.29
).

To see why (
5.29
) holds, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{2T+2}{W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{F}}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{2}\prod_{j=T+1}^{2T+2}{W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\left.\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{y},(-\infty)^{2T+2};2,2T+2}\left[\mathcal{R}_{2}\prod_{j=1}^{T-1}{W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}\mid\mathcal{G}\right]\right].

By the Gibbs property, the inner expectation, when viewed as a random variable, is almost surely equal to
\mathcal{V}_{2}
defined in (
2.8
). On the other hand, we have

\prod_{j=T+1}^{2T+2}{W(z_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1))}=\prod_{j=1}^{2T+2}W(w_{j};L_{2}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j-1)).

Combining the above two observations, leads to (
5.29
).

5.3.
Proof of Proposition
5.2

As with the proof of Propositions
3.9
and
4.1
, we divide the proof of Proposition
5.2
into two parts depending on
p=1
(critical) or
p=2
(supercritical).

Proof of Proposition
5.2
in the
p=1
case (critical phase).

Fix any
T\in\llbracket k_{1}N^{\frac{2}{3}},k_{2}N^{\frac{2}{3}}\rrbracket
. Fix any
\delta\leq\gamma/{6\kappa}
. We recall the representation of bottom-free law in
p=1
case from Lemma
4.3
. Consider the Brownian motion
B_{1}
obtained via the KMT coupling that satisfies (
4.9
). Define

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\in\llbracket 1,T\rrbracket\\
|i_{1}-i_{2}|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}N^{2/3}\end{subarray}}|L_{1}(2i_{1}-1)-L_{1}(2i_{2}-1)|\geq\tfrac{1}{6}\gamma N^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}(k)

\displaystyle:=\left\{|L_{1}(2k-1)-L_{1}(2k)|,|L_{1}(2k+1)-L_{1}(2k)|\geq\tfrac{1}{3}\gamma N^{\frac{1}{3}}\right\}.

Fix any
x\in\mathbb{R}
and write
\mathbb{P}_{1}:=\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{1}}^{x,(-\infty)^{T};1,T}
. Observe that by union bound we have

(5.30)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{1}\left(\omega_{\delta}^{N}(L_{1},\llbracket 1,2T-1\rrbracket)\geq\gamma N^{1/3}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{1}(\mathsf{A}_{\delta})+\sum_{k=1}^{T-1}\mathbb{P}_{1}(\neg\mathsf{A}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{B}(k)).

We now proceed to bound each of the above term separately. For the first term, by (
4.7
) and (
4.9
), in view of the estimate in (
4.10
) we have for all large enough
N
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{1}(\mathsf{A}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{1}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\in\llbracket 1,T\rrbracket\\
|i_{1}-i_{2}|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}N^{2/3}\end{subarray}}\sigma|B_{1}{(T-i_{1}-1)}-B_{1}{(T-i_{2}-1)}|\geq\tfrac{\gamma}{12}N^{1/3}-2\mathrm{C}\log T\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{1}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\in\llbracket 1,T\rrbracket\\
|i_{1}-i_{2}|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}N^{2/3}\end{subarray}}\sigma|B_{1}{(i_{1})}-B_{1}{(i_{2})}|\geq\tfrac{\gamma}{24}N^{1/3}\bigg).

By modulus of continuity of Brownian motion, the right-hand side of the above equation can be made smaller than
\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon
by choosing
\delta
small enough depending on
\mu,\theta,\gamma,k_{1},k_{2}
. For the second term in r.h.s. (
5.30
) we use Lemma
B.4
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{1}(\neg\mathsf{A}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{B}(k))\leq\mathrm{C}e^{-\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}}\gamma N^{\frac{1}{3}}}.

Plugging the bounds back in (
5.30
) and taking
N
large enough we get the desired result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2
in the
p=2
case (supercritical phase).

Fix any
(x_{1},x_{2})\in I_{2,M}
, and
T\in\llbracket k_{1}N^{\frac{2}{3}},k_{2}N^{\frac{2}{3}}\rrbracket
. Set
n:=T
. Recall the law paired random walk and weighted paired random walk defined in Definition
1.7
. We recall from Lemma
4.4
that the bottom-free law
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{(x_{1},x_{2}),(-\infty)^{T};2,T}
is equal to
{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{WPRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}
for the supercritical case. At this point is it also good to recall the random walk measures from Definition
3.6
.

A key to this proof is the following estimate for
{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]
(recall
W_{\operatorname{sc}}
from (
1.15
)).

Lemma 5.6
.

There exist constants
\mathrm{C}_{1},\mathrm{C}_{2}>0
, depending on
M
, such that for all
(x_{1},x_{2})\in I_{2,M}
we have

(5.31)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}})\geq{\mathrm{C}_{2}^{-1}}e^{-\mathrm{C}_{2}(\log n)^{5/4}},

where
\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}}:=\{S_{1}(k)\geq S_{2}(k)\mbox{ for all }k\in\llbracket 1,n/4\rrbracket\}
and
S_{1},S_{2}
are random walks under the law
\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}
.

Before proving Lemma
5.6
we complete the proof of Proposition
5.2
in the following two steps.

Step 1.
Fix any
V,\gamma>0
. Set
v=\gamma/\sqrt{k_{2}},u=V/\sqrt{k_{1}}
, and
t=\lfloor 2\log\log n\rfloor
. Let
\mathcal{F}:=\sigma(S_{1}(1),S_{2}(1))
.
Consider the events

\displaystyle\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{|S_{1}(1)|+|S_{2}(1)|\leq u\sqrt{n},\omega_{\delta}^{N}(S_{i}(\cdot),\llbracket 0,\tfrac{n}{8}\rrbracket)\geq\tfrac{1}{6}v\sqrt{n},\mbox{ for }i=1,2\Big\},\mbox{ for }\delta>0.

We claim that given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta
small enough and
N
large enough such that

(5.32)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{WPRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta})\leq\varepsilon.

We finish the proof of the lemma assuming (
5.32
). Lemma
4.4
implies that
\big(L_{1}(2j+1),L_{2}(2j+2)\big)_{j=0}^{n}
is distributed as
\mathsf{WPRW}
. Write
L_{2}^{\mathsf{even}}(k):=L_{2}(2k)
and
\mathbb{P}_{2}:=\mathbb{P}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\vec{x},(-\infty)^{T};2,T}
. Then (
5.32
) implies

(5.33)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}\big(\{|L_{1}(1)|+|L_{2}(2)|\leq VN^{1/3}\}\cap\mathsf{A}\big)\leq\varepsilon,\quad\mathsf{A}:=\Big\{\omega_{\delta}^{N}(L_{2}^{\mathsf{even}},\llbracket 1,T/8\rrbracket)\geq\tfrac{1}{6}\gamma N^{1/3}\Big\}.

On the event
\neg\mathsf{A}
the increments of
L_{2}^{\mathsf{even}}
are well controlled. By Lemma
4.4
, conditioned on the even points of
L_{2}
, the distribution of the odd points of
L_{2}
are given by
\xi
-distributions defined in (
4.5
). Once we have a bound on the increments of
L_{2}^{\mathsf{even}}
, we may invoke the tails estimates of
\xi
-distributions from Lemma
B.4
to control increments of
L_{2}
. In particular, due to Lemma
B.4
,

\mathbf{1}_{\neg\mathsf{A}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{2}\left[\mathbf{1}_{|L_{2}(2k+1)-L_{2}(2k)|,|L_{2}(2k+1)-L_{2}(2k+2)|\geq\frac{1}{3}\gamma N^{1/3}}\mid\sigma\big(L_{2}^{\mathsf{even}}\llbracket 1,T/8\rrbracket\big)\right]\leq\mathrm{C}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}}\gamma N^{1/3})

for all
k\geq 1
. For the first point in
L_{2}
, i.e.,
L_{2}(1)
, we recall from Lemma
4.4
that
L_{2}(1)\sim X+L_{2}(2)
where
X\sim G_{\alpha_{2}+\theta,1}
. The explicit form of
G_{\alpha_{2}+\theta,1}
from (
2.2
) allow us to derive that

\mathbb{P}_{2}\bigg(|L_{2}(1)-L_{2}(2)|\geq\tfrac{1}{6}\gamma N^{1/3}\mid\sigma\big(L_{2}^{\mathsf{even}}\llbracket 1,T/8\rrbracket\big)\bigg)\leq\mathrm{C}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}}\gamma N^{1/3}).

Thus, in view of (
5.33
), by the union bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}\left(|L_{1}(1)|+|L_{2}(2)|\leq VN^{\frac{1}{3}},\omega_{\delta}^{N}(L_{2},\llbracket 1,T/4\rrbracket)\geq\gamma N^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\leq\varepsilon+\mathrm{C}\cdot k_{2}N^{\frac{2}{3}}\exp(-\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}}\gamma N^{\frac{1}{3}})

which can be made arbitrarily small taking
N
large enough. A similar argument shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2}\left(|L_{1}(1)|+|L_{2}(2)|\leq VN^{\frac{1}{3}},\omega_{\delta}^{N}(L_{1},\llbracket 1,T/4-1\rrbracket)\geq\gamma N^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)

can be made arbitrarily small as well taking
N
large enough. This proves Proposition
5.2
.

Step 2.
In this step we prove (
5.32
). First, recall that due to (
1.14
),

{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{WPRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta})=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}}]}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]}

where
W_{\operatorname{sc}}
is defined in (
1.15
).
We first define a few more necessary events.

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\{|S_{1}(1)|+|S_{2}(1)|\leq u\sqrt{n},|S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1)|\leq(\log n)^{3/2}\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\{|S_{1}(1)|+|S_{2}(1)|\leq u\sqrt{n},1\leq S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1)\leq 2\}.

Recall the non-intersection event
\mathsf{NI}_{p}
from (
4.30
). Let us temporarily set
t=\lceil 2\log\log n\rceil
. As
W_{\operatorname{sc}}\leq 1
, we write

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}}]\leq\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{G}_{1}\cap\mathsf{NI}_{t}}]}_{(\mathbf{I})}+\underbrace{{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\neg\mathsf{NI}_{t}}]+{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[\mathbf{1}_{\neg\mathsf{G}_{1}}]}}_{(\mathbf{II})}.

For
(\mathbf{II})
, note that on
\neg\mathsf{NI}_{t}
, we have
W_{\operatorname{sc}}\leq e^{-e^{t}}\leq e^{-(\log n)^{2}}
and by Lemma
4.7
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\neg\mathsf{G}_{1})\leq\mathrm{C}e^{-\mathrm{C}^{-1}(\log n)^{3/2}}
. Thus,
(\mathbf{II})\leq\mathrm{C}e^{-\mathrm{C}^{-1}(\log n)^{3/2}}
. In view of Lemma
5.6
,
({\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}])^{-1}\cdot(\mathbf{II})\to 0
. For
(\mathbf{I})
, note that

(5.34)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{I})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{G}_{1}\cap\mathsf{NI}_{0}}]+\sum_{p=1}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{G}_{1}\cap\mathsf{NI}_{p}\cap\neg\mathsf{NI}_{p-1}}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p=0}^{t}\mathrm{C}e^{-e^{p}}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{NI}_{p}}\mid\mathcal{F}]\big].

Upon conditioning on
\mathcal{F}
, the conditional law is the law of two independent
n
-step random bridges from
(S_{1}(1),S_{2}(1))
to
(x_{1},x_{2})
. We may lift the bridges by
p
units. The modulus of continuity event remains unchanged and
\mathsf{NI}_{p}
event turns into
\mathsf{NI}
. Now we apply the comparison trick between random bridges and modified random bridges via Lemma
4.10
. By Lemma
4.10
, there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}
depending only on
u
such that

(5.35)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{NI}_{p}}\mid\mathcal{F}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\mathbb{P}^{n;(S_{1}(1),S_{2}(1)),(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{NI}_{p})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\mathbb{P}^{n;(S_{1}(1)+p,S_{2}(1)),(x_{1}+p,x_{2})}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{NI})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{C}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\cap\mathsf{NI})=\mathrm{C}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\mid\mathsf{NI})\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{NI})

where
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}
denote the law of a
(n;\lfloor n/4\rfloor,\lfloor n/4\rfloor)
-modified random bridge defined in Definition
4.9
starting from
(S_{1}(1)+p,S_{2}(1))
to
(x_{1}+p,x_{2})
. Observe that
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{NI})
is
\mathcal{F}
-measurable. By Lemmas
C.3
and
C.8
(recall Corollary
4.6
),

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{NI})\leq\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\cdot\tfrac{\mathrm{C}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot e^{\mathrm{C}p}\cdot\max\{S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1),1\}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor,(x_{1},x_{2})}\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}}\big).

We plug the estimates from (
5.35
) and (
5.36
) back in (
5.34
). Thus setting
\mathrm{C}_{3}:=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}2\mathrm{C}_{1}\mathrm{C}^{3}e^{\mathrm{C}r}e^{-e^{r}}
(with
\mathrm{C}_{1}
coming from Lemma
5.6
) and utilizing the lower bound for
{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]
from Lemma
5.6
, we have

\displaystyle({\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}])^{-1}\cdot(\mathbf{I})\leq\mathrm{C}_{3}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\cdot\max\{S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1),1\}\cdot\sup_{p\in\llbracket 0,t\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\mid\mathsf{NI})\right]

Now we claim that one can choose
\delta
sufficiently small such that

(5.37)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}\cdot\max\{S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1),1\}\cdot\sup_{p\in\llbracket 0,t\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\mid\mathsf{NI})\right]\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}\varepsilon.

We write
\mathsf{G}_{1}=\mathsf{G}_{1,M_{2}}\cup\widetilde{\mathsf{G}}_{1,M_{2}}
, where

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{1,M_{2}}:=\{|S_{1}(1)|+|S_{2}(1)|\leq u\sqrt{n},|S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1)|\leq M_{2}\},\quad\widetilde{\mathsf{G}}_{1,M_{2}}:=\mathsf{G}_{1}\cap\neg\mathsf{G}_{1,M_{2}}.

Given the tail estimates, one can choose
M_{2}
large enough such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{G}}_{1,M_{2}}}\cdot\max\{S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1),1\}\right]\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}\varepsilon.

This fixes our choice for
M_{2}
. Now note that the event
\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}
depends only on the first
\lfloor n/8\rfloor
points of the two
(n;\lfloor n/4\rfloor,\lfloor n/4\rfloor)
-modified random bridges. By definition, the first
\lfloor n/4\rfloor
points of a
(n;\lfloor n/4\rfloor,\lfloor n/4\rfloor)
-modified random bridge is just a random walk. Thus, in view of Lemma
C.12
(recall Corollary
4.6
), one can then choose
\delta
small enough and
N
large enough such that on uniformly on
\mathsf{G}_{1,M_{2}}
we have

\sup_{p\in\llbracket 0,t\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\mid\mathsf{NI})\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}M_{2}^{-1}\varepsilon.

Thus,
we have

l.h.s. (
5.37
)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{G}}_{1,M_{2}}}\!\!\!\cdot\max\{S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1),1\}\Big]+M_{2}\!\cdot\!{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{1,M_{2}}}\!\cdot\!\sup_{p\in\llbracket 0,t\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{p}(\mathsf{MC}_{\delta}\mid\mathsf{NI})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}\varepsilon+M_{2}\!\cdot\!\tfrac{1}{4}\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}M_{2}^{-1}\varepsilon=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}\varepsilon,

verifying the inequality in (
5.37
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Recall the definition of
(n,p,q)
-modified random bridge from Definition
4.9
, in particular that
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(n;\lfloor n/4\rfloor,\lfloor n/4\rfloor);(a_{1},a_{2}),(x_{1},x_{2})}
denotes the law of two independent
(n,\lfloor n/4\rfloor,\lfloor n/4\rfloor)
-modified random bridge started at
(a_{1},a_{2})
and ended at
(x_{1},x_{2})
. We shall use the shorthand
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(a_{1},a_{2})}
for
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(n;\lfloor n/4\rfloor,\lfloor n/4\rfloor);(a_{1},a_{2}),(x_{1},x_{2})}
. Also recall the notation
\mathbb{P}^{m;(b_{1},b_{2})}
from Definition
3.6
to denote the law of two independent random walks of length
m
started at
(b_{1},b_{2})
with same increment law as the modified bridges.

Recall the events
\mathsf{NI}
and
\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}
from (
4.30
) and (
4.22
) respectively. Invoking Lemma
C.11
we first fix a
\beta=\beta(M)\leq\frac{1}{2}
small enough so that it satisfies

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(a_{1},a_{2})}(\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\mid\mathsf{NI})\geq\tfrac{3}{4},

for all
|a_{i}|\leq\sqrt{n}
with
1\leq a_{1}-a_{2}\leq 2
. Next by Lemma
C.5
, we fix
\xi=\xi(M)>0
so that

\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(b_{1},b_{2})}\Big(|S_{1}(\lfloor n/4\rfloor)|,|S_{2}(\lfloor n/4\rfloor)|\leq\xi\sqrt{n}\mid\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}}\Big)\geq\sqrt{\tfrac{3}{4}}

for all
|b_{i}|\leq(M+1)\sqrt{n}
. Here
\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}}:=\{S_{1}(k)\geq S_{2}(k)\mbox{ for all }k\in\llbracket 2,n/4\rrbracket\}
is the non-intersection event over
\lfloor n/4\rfloor
points.

We consider the following events

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|S_{i}(1)|\leq\sqrt{n}\mbox{ for }i=1,2,\ 1\leq S_{1}(1)-S_{2}(1)\leq 2\big\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\xi}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|S_{i}(\lfloor n/4\rfloor)|,|S_{i}(n-\lfloor n/4\rfloor)|\leq\xi\sqrt{n}\mbox{ for }i=1,2\big\},

where
\mathsf{T}
stands for tightness. Observe that by Lemma
4.8
we have

(5.38)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]\geq{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{G}_{3}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi}}]

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}}{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{G}_{3}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{C}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta},\mathsf{T}_{\xi}}\mid\mathcal{F}]\right]

where
\mathcal{F}:=\sigma(S_{1}(1),S_{2}(1))
. Under the event
\mathsf{G}_{3}
and
\mathsf{T}_{\xi}
we may invoke Lemma
4.10
to get

(5.39)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi}}\mid\mathcal{F}]\geq\mathrm{C}^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}(\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi})

almost surely. By Corollary
C.10
(recall Corollary
4.6
),

(5.40)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}({\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}({\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi}}\mid\mathsf{NI})\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(a_{1},a_{2})}(\mathsf{NI})

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{C}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}({\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta}\cap\mathsf{T}_{\xi}}\mid\mathsf{NI})\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}})\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}}).

By our choice of
\beta
and
\xi
, we have
\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}({\mathsf{Gap}_{\beta},\mathsf{T}_{\xi}}\mid\mathsf{NI})\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}
almost surely. By Lemma
C.3
(recall Corollary
4.6
), we have
\mathbf{1}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(S_{1}(1),S_{1}(2))}(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}})\geq\frac{\mathrm{C}^{-1}}{\sqrt{n}}
almost surely. Thus combining (
5.38
), (
5.39
), and (
5.40
) we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}[W_{\operatorname{sc}}]\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathrm{C}^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{G}_{3}).

By Lemma
4.7
((
4.17
) in particular),
{\mathbb{P}}_{\operatorname{PRW}}^{n;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{G}_{3})\geq\mathrm{C}^{-1}
. Plugging this back in the above equation we get the first inequality in (
5.31
). For the second inequality, we consider the event:

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{4}

\displaystyle:=\{|S_{1}(2)-x_{1}|\leq 1,\,\,|S_{2}(2)-\min\{x_{1}-3,x_{2}\}|\leq 1\}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\widetilde{\mathsf{NI}})\geq\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}\bigg(\mathsf{G}_{4}\cap\{S_{1}(j)\geq S_{2}(j)\mbox{ for all }j\in\llbracket 3,n/4\rrbracket\}\bigg).

By the tail bounds of the increments from Lemma
B.3
, and given the condition
x_{1}-x_{2}\geq-(\log N)^{7/6}
, we have
\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}(\mathsf{G}_{4})\geq\mathrm{C}^{-1}\exp(-\mathrm{C}(\log n)^{7/6})
(recall
n\geq k_{1}N^{2/3}-1
). Furthermore, on
\mathsf{G}_{4}
we must have
S_{1}(2)\geq S_{2}(2)
. By Lemma
C.3
(recall Corollary
4.6
), we have

\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor-1;(a_{1},a_{2})}\big(S_{1}(j)\geq S_{2}(j)\mbox{ for all }j\in\llbracket 2,n/4-1\rrbracket\big)\geq\mathrm{C}^{-1}/\sqrt{n}

for all
a_{1}\geq a_{2}
. Thus we have

\mathbb{P}^{\lfloor n/4\rfloor;(x_{1},x_{2})}\bigg(\mathsf{G}_{4}\cap\{S_{1}(j)\geq S_{2}(j)\mbox{ for all }j\in\llbracket 3,n/4\rrbracket\}\bigg)\geq\mathrm{C}^{-1}\exp(-\mathrm{C}(\log n)^{7/6})\cdot\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.

Adjusting the constant we get the second inequality in (
5.31
). ∎

Appendix A
Stochastic monotonicity

The goal of this section is to prove the stochastic monotonicity of
\mathpzc{HSLG}
Gibbs measure (Proposition
2.6
). Let
\Lambda=\{(i,j):k_{1}\leq i\leq k_{2},a_{i}\leq j\leq b_{i}\}
. Let
w_{1},\ldots,w_{|\Lambda|}
be the enumeration of points in
\Lambda
in the lexicographic order. Set
\Lambda_{r}=\{w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{r}\}
, so that
\Lambda_{|\Lambda|}=\Lambda
. Let
E_{r}:=E(\Lambda_{r}\cup\partial\Lambda_{r})
(the edges in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
connecting points in
\Lambda_{r}\cup\partial\Lambda_{r}
), and, recalling the weights
W_{e}
from (
1.5
), let

(A.1)

H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{|\Lambda_{r-1}|}}\prod_{e=\{v_{1}\to v_{2}\}\in E_{r}}W_{e}(u_{v_{1}}-u_{v_{2}})\prod_{v\in\Lambda_{r-1}}du_{v},

where
u_{w_{r}}=x
. The proof of Proposition
2.6
relies on the following technical lemma.

Lemma A.1
.

Fix
r\in\llbracket 1,|\Lambda|\rrbracket
. For each
v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}
, fix any
u_{v},u_{v}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
with
u_{v}\leq u_{v}^{\prime}
. For all
s\geq t

(A.2)

\displaystyle H_{r}\big(s;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}\big)H_{r}\big(t;(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}\big)\leq H_{r}\big(s;(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}\big)H_{r}\big(t;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}\big)

We prove Lemma
A.1
at the end of this section and now complete the proof of Proposition
2.6
.

Proof of Proposition
2.6
.

Fix
r\in\llbracket 1,|\Lambda|\rrbracket
. We first claim that for all boundary conditions
(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}
and
(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}
with
u_{v}\leq u_{v}^{\prime}
for all
v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}
, and
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

(A.3)

\mathbb{P}\big(L(w_{r})\leq s\mid L(v)=u_{v}\textrm{ for all }v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big(L(w_{r})\leq s\mid L(v)=u_{v}^{\prime}\textrm{ for all }v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}\big).

To show this, observe that
H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})
in (
A.1
) is proportional to the conditional density at
x
of
L(w_{r})
given
\big(L(v)\big)_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}=(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}
. Thus,

(A.4)

\mathbb{P}\big(L(w_{r})\leq s\mid L(v)=u_{v}\textrm{ for all }v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}\big)=F_{r}\big(s;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}}\big):=\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{s}H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx}{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx}

To prove (
A.3
) observe that owing to Lemma
A.1
, the derivative of

\log\int_{-\infty}^{s}H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx-\log\int_{-\infty}^{s}H_{r}(x;(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx.

is non-positive for all
s
. This implies for
s^{\prime}\geq s
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{s}H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx}{\int_{-\infty}^{s}H_{r}(x;(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx}\geq\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{s^{\prime}}H_{r}(x;(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx}{\int_{-\infty}^{s^{\prime}}H_{r}(x;(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r}})dx}

Taking
s^{\prime}\to\infty
and cross-multiplying yields the desired inequality (
A.3
), in light of (
A.4
).

Given
(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\partial\Lambda|}
, we now define a sequence of random variables according to the following algorithm. Note that below,
x\leftarrow y
means to assign the value
y
to the variable
x
.

Algorithm 1

Defining the random vectors

Generate
U_{1},\ldots,U_{|\Lambda|}
i.i.d. random variables from
U[0,1]

Y_{|\Lambda|}\leftarrow(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}

r\leftarrow|\Lambda|

while

r\geq 1

do

L(w_{r};(u_{w})_{v\in\partial\Lambda})\leftarrow F_{r}^{-1}(U_{r};Y_{r})

\widetilde{u}_{v}\leftarrow u_{v}
for all
v\in\partial\Lambda_{r-1}\cap\partial\Lambda_{r}

\widetilde{u}_{w_{r}}\leftarrow L(w_{r};(u_{w})_{v\in\partial\Lambda})

Y_{r-1}\leftarrow(\widetilde{u}_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda_{r-1}}

r\leftarrow r-1

end

while

This defines a collection of random variables

L(w_{i};(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda})
indexed by
i\in\llbracket 1,|\Lambda|\rrbracket
and
(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\partial\Lambda|}
,
all on the common probability space on which
U_{1},\ldots,U_{|\Lambda|}
are defined. It is clear from the definition that for each
(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\partial\Lambda|}
, the law of
\big(L(w_{i};(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda})\big)_{i\in\llbracket 1,|\Lambda|\rrbracket}
is given by the
\mathpzc{HSLG}
Gibbs measure on the domain
\Lambda
with boundary condition
(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}
. Take two boundary conditions
(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}
and
(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda}
with
u_{v}\leq u_{v}^{\prime}
for all
v\in\partial\Lambda
. As each
F_{r}
is stochastically increasing with respect to the boundary condition, i.e., (
A.3
), sequentially we obtain that with probability 1 on our probability space
L(w_{r};(u_{v})_{v\in\partial\Lambda})\leq L(w_{r};(u_{v}^{\prime})_{v\in\partial\Lambda})
for all
r
, thus completing the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

Let us begin with a few pieces of notations. Fix any
1\leq r\leq|\Lambda|
. Set
e_{r}:=\{w_{r}\to(w_{r}+(0,1)),(w_{r}+(0,1))\to w_{r}\}\cap E_{r}
. In words, this is the directed blue edge (see Figure
19
A) with
w_{r}
as the left point of
e_{r}
.

w_{2}

w_{4}

(a)
